The non-maximal symmetry breaking patterns in the supersymmetric 𝔰 ​ 𝔲 ^ ​ ( 8 ) k U = 1 \widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1} theory
Ning Chen, Jianan Tian, Bin Wang

Abstract
We study the non-maximal symmetry breaking patterns of 𝔰 ​ 𝔲 ​ ( 8 ) → 𝔤 531 / 𝔤 351 {\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}/\mathfrak{g}_{351} allowed in a supersymmetric theory with the affine Lie algebra of 𝔰 ​ 𝔲 ^ ​ ( 8 ) k U = 1 \widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1} , which was recently proposed to explain the three-generational standard model quark/lepton mass hierarchies as well as the Cabbibo-Kobayashi-Maskawa mixing pattern. Three gauge couplings are found to achieve the unification below the Planck scales, according to the unification relations determined by the affine Lie algebra at level of 1 1 . We also prove that a separate non-maximal symmetry breaking pattern of 𝔰 ​ 𝔲 ​ ( 8 ) → 𝔤 621 {\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{621} is unrealistic due to the additional massless vectorlike quarks in the spectrum.

Introduction

Grand unified theories (GUTs) were proposed to not only unify all three Standard Model (SM) symmetries into one simple Lie algebra of either
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}(5)

[
1
]
or
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{o}(10)

[
2
]
, but to also unify all SM fermions into some anomaly-free irreducible representations (IRs) of some simple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{U}
.
The Georgi-Glashow
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}(5)
GUT
[
1
]
and the Fritzsch-Minkowski
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{o}(10)
GUT
[
2
]
contain the chiral fermions of
3\times\left[\mathbf{\overline{5_{F}}}\oplus\mathbf{10_{F}}\right]
and
3\times\mathbf{16_{F}}
, respectively.
Neither framework can fully explain the three-generational SM fermion mass hierarchies and the Cabibbo-Kobayashi-Maskawa (CKM) mixing pattern
[
3
,
4
]
due to three repetitive chiral irreducible anomaly-free fermion sets (IRAFFSs)
1
1

1

The definition of the chiral IRAFFS was previously given in Refs.
[
5
,
6
]
.
in the flavor sector.

In a seminal paper by Georgi
[
7
]
, he first proposed to avoid the repetitive structure in the flavor sector in a unified
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(11)
theory.
This was recently relaxed with two following chiral IRAFFSs at the GUT scale:

\displaystyle\{f_{L}\}_{{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)}^{n_{g}=3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\omega}\oplus\mathbf{28_{F}}\Big]\bigoplus\Big[\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}}\oplus\mathbf{56_{F}}\Big]\,,\penalty\ {\rm dim}_{\mathbf{F}}=156\,,

(1)

\displaystyle\Omega\equiv(\omega\,,\dot{\omega})\,,\penalty\ \omega=(3\,,{\rm IV}\,,{\rm V}\,,{\rm VI})\,,\penalty\ \dot{\omega}=(\dot{1}\,,\dot{2}\,,\dot{\rm VII}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}\,,\dot{\rm IX})\,,

starting from a non-supersymmetric
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)
theory
[
8
,
5
,
6
]
.
Here, the undotted/dotted indices represent the
\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}
’s in the rank-two chiral IRAFFS and the rank-three chiral IRAFFS
2
2

2

The rank-two and the rank-three chiral IRAFFSs are named after the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)
rank-two and rank-three antisymmetric fermions of
\mathbf{28_{F}}
and
\mathbf{56_{F}}
, respectively.
, respectively.
We also distinguish the heavy partner fermions and the SM fermions in terms of the Roman numbers and the Arabic numbers.
The global symmetries of the chiral fermions in Eq. (
1
) are

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\rm global}\left[{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\,,n_{g}=3\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\widetilde{{\rm SU}}(4)_{\omega}\otimes\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T_{2}}\otimes\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{{\rm PQ}_{2}}\Big]\bigotimes\Big[\widetilde{{\rm SU}}(5)_{\dot{\omega}}\otimes\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T_{3}}\otimes\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{{\rm PQ}_{3}}\Big]\,,

(2)

with the
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T_{i}}
being the non-anomalous global symmetries, and the
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{{\rm PQ}_{i}}
being the anomalous global Peccei-Quinn symmetries
[
9
]
.
To count the SM generations by decomposing the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)
fermion IRs into the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(5)
IRs
[
7
]
, one finds three identical SM
\mathbf{\overline{5_{F}}}
’s and three distinctive
\mathbf{10_{F}}
’s
[
8
]
.
It is sufficient to obtain three distinctive SM generations in the UV setup of the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)
theory.

The non-supersymmetric
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)
theory were analyzed according to its maximal symmetry breaking pattern of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{441}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{0}}

[
6
,
10
]
, which was favored by the minimization condition of the adjoint Higgs potential
[
11
]
.
It was also found that the renormalization group equations (RGEs) of the gauge couplings in the non-supersymmetric
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)
theory cannot achieve the unification in the field theoretical interpretation of
\alpha_{4s}(v_{U})=\alpha_{4W}(v_{U})=\alpha_{1}(v_{U})

[
8
]
, even if the one-loop threshold effects
[
12
,
13
,
14
]
and the higher-dimension operators were taken into account.
The main difficulties of achieving the gauge coupling unification are: (i) the Cartan discontinuities of different Abelian gauge couplings through the symmetry breaking stage of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(N)\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X}\to{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(N-1)\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X^{\prime}}
given by

\displaystyle\alpha_{X^{\prime}}^{-1}=\frac{2}{N(N-1)}\alpha_{N}^{-1}+\alpha_{X}^{-1}\,,

(3)

where
(\alpha_{N}\,,\alpha_{X^{\prime}}\,,\alpha_{X})
are the gauge couplings of the
({\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(N)\,,{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X}\,,{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X^{\prime}})
symmetries, and (ii) the suggested intermediate symmetry breaking scales, which were previously determined according to the observed SM quark/lepton masses and the CKM mixing patterns
[
6
]
.
In the context of string theory, it was shown by Ginsparg
[
15
]
that the gauge coupling unification can be achieved as

\displaystyle k_{s}\alpha_{s}(v_{U})=k_{W}\alpha_{W}(v_{U})=k_{1}\alpha_{1}(v_{U})\,,

(4)

with
(k_{s}\,,k_{W}\,,k_{1})
being the affine levels of the corresponding strong, weak, and the Abelian gauge couplings in the physical basis
3
3

3

Some early studies of the higher affine levels in the string-inspired unified theories include Refs.
[
16
,
17
]
.
.
In a recent paper
[

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{s\,,k_{s}=1}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{W\,,k_{W}=1}\oplus\hat{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{1\,,k_{1\,,{\tt phys}}=\frac{1}{4}}\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1}\,,

(5)

can achieve the gauge coupling unification with the
\mathcal{N}=1
supersymmetric (SUSY) extension between the scale of
v_{441}\leq\mu\leq v_{U}
.

It turns out the Yukawa coupling terms of
\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\omega}\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}
,
\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}
, and
\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{63_{H}}\mathbf{28_{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}
give vectorlike fermion masses through the sequential stages after the breaking of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{441}

[
5
]
, leaving three generational massless SM quarks/leptons plus twenty-three left-handed sterile neutrinos before the EWSB stage according to the ‘t Hooft anomaly matching conditions
[
19
]
.
Furthermore, the previous analyses of the maximal symmetry breaking patterns
[
5
,
6
,
20
]
reveal the origins of three-generational SM quark/lepton masses and the observed Cabibbo-Kobayashi-Maskawa (CKM) mixing pattern
[
3
,
4
]
in the quark sector through the Yukawa coupling to one unique SM Higgs boson within the
\mathbf{70_{H}}
in the spectrum.
Specifically, the Yukawa coupling of
\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{70_{H}}
in Eq. (
6
) leads to the top quark mass, and other SM up-type quark masses are due to two
d=5
operators of

\displaystyle c_{4}\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}^{(4\,,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{4}\,\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\cdot\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}\cdot\mathbf{70_{H}}\,,

(7a)

\displaystyle c_{5}\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}^{(5\,,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{5}\,\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\cdot\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}\cdot\mathbf{70_{H}}\,.

(7b)

To generate the masses for all down-type quarks and charged leptons, we conjecture two sets of Higgs mixing terms

\displaystyle d_{\mathscr{A}}\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{A}}^{d=5}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d_{\mathscr{A}}\,\epsilon_{\omega_{1}\omega_{2}\omega_{3}\omega_{4}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega_{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega_{2}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega_{3}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega_{4}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{70_{H}}^{\dagger}\,,\quad\mathcal{P}\mathcal{Q}=2(2p+3q_{2})\neq 0\,,

(8a)

\displaystyle d_{\mathscr{B}}\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{B}}^{d=5}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d_{\mathscr{B}}\,(\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\kappa}_{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\kappa}_{2}})\cdot\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{70_{H}}^{\dagger}\,,\quad\mathcal{P}\mathcal{Q}=2(p+q_{2}+q_{3})\,,

(8b)

\displaystyle{\rm with}\penalty\ \penalty\ \dot{\kappa}_{2}\neq(\dot{\kappa}_{1}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2})\,,

where one of the Higgs fields will mix with two renormalizable Yukawa couplings of
Y_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\omega}\mathbf{28_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}+Y_{\mathcal{D}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}+{\rm H.c.}
in Eq. (
6
).
Both
d=5
Yukawa couplings in Eqs. (
7
) and Higgs mixing terms in Eqs. (
8
) violate the global symmetries in Eq. (
2
) explicitly, and they were conjectured to be suppressed by the reduced Planck scale of
M_{\rm pl}=(8\pi G_{N})^{-\frac{1}{2}}=2.4\times 10^{18}\,{\rm GeV}
.
Three intermediate symmetry breaking scales beyond the EW scale enter into the SM quark/lepton mass matrices, and hence are set by requiring a reasonable matching with the experimental measurements in the flavor sector.

It was previously pointed out by Witten that other non-maximal symmetry breaking patterns are possible in a SUSY theory
[
21
]
.
The superpotential for the adjoint field
\mathbf{63_{H}}
takes the form of

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mu_{U}\Tr( \rep{63_H} )^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\lambda_{U}\Tr( \rep{63_H} )^{3}\,,

(9)

and the corresponding field equation for the unbroken SUSY reads

\displaystyle\lambda_{U}(\mathbf{63_{H}}^{2}-\frac{1}{8}\mathbb{I}_{8}\Tr( \rep{63_H} )^{2})+\mu_{U}\mathbf{63_{H}}=0\,.

(10)

The vacuum expectation values (VEVs) of the adjoint Higgs field and the corresponding symmetry breaking patterns are

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{441}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\mathbf{63_{H}}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}v_{U}{\rm diag}(\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{I}_{4}\,,-\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{I}_{4})\,,

(11a)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}/\mathfrak{g}_{351}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\mathbf{63_{H}}\rangle=\frac{3\mu_{U}}{2\lambda_{U}}{\rm diag}(\mathbb{I}_{5}\,,-\frac{5}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3})/\langle\mathbf{63_{H}}\rangle=\frac{5\mu_{U}}{2\lambda_{U}}{\rm diag}(\mathbb{I}_{3}\,,-\frac{3}{5}\mathbb{I}_{5})\,,

(11b)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{621}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\mathbf{63_{H}}\rangle=\frac{\mu_{U}}{2\lambda_{U}}{\rm diag}(\mathbb{I}_{6}\,,-3\mathbb{I}_{2})\,.

(11c)

The maximal symmetry breaking pattern of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{441}
can be achieved with the vanishing mass term of
\mu_{U}
in Eq. (
9
), and have been recently studied in Refs.
[
6
,
10
,
20
,
18
]
.
As for the non-maximal symmetry breaking patterns in Eqs. (
11b
) and (
11c
), we have also proved in Ref.
[
18
]
that the corresponding conformal embeddings are

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(5)_{k_{s/W}=1}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{k_{W/s}=1}\oplus\hat{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{k_{1\,,{\tt phys}}=\frac{1}{4}}\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1}\,,

(12a)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(6)_{k_{s}=1}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(2)_{k_{W}=1}\oplus\hat{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{k_{1\,,{\tt phys}}=\frac{1}{4}}\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1}\,.

(12b)

Given the possible non-maximal symmetry breaking patterns in the SUSY extension, as well as the conformal embeddings in Eqs. (
12a
) and (
12b
), it is a central question to check whether the gauge coupling unification can be achieved according to Eq. (
4
).

If one considers the non-maximal symmetry breaking patterns of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}
or
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{351}
at the GUT scale, there can be four possible sequential symmetry breaking patterns
5
5

5

The acronyms stand for the strong-strong-weak (SSW), strong-weak-strong (SWS), weak-strong-strong (WSS), and weak-weak-weak (WWW) symmetry breaking patterns, respectively.
, namely,

\displaystyle{\rm SSW}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}\to\mathfrak{g}_{431}\to\mathfrak{g}_{331}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}\,,

(13a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{531}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(5)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{0}}\,,\,\mathfrak{g}_{331}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{c}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{\rm SWS}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}\to\mathfrak{g}_{431}\to\mathfrak{g}_{421}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}\,,

(13b)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{431}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{1}}\,,\,\mathfrak{g}_{421}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(2)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{\rm WSS}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}\to\mathfrak{g}_{521}\to\mathfrak{g}_{421}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}\,,

(13c)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{521}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(5)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(2)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{1}}\,,

\displaystyle{\rm WWW}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{351}\to\mathfrak{g}_{341}\to\mathfrak{g}_{331}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}\,,

(13d)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{351}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{c}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(5)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{0}}\,,\,\mathfrak{g}_{341}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{c}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{1}}\,,

with
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{c}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(2)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{Y}
.
In contrast to the maximal symmetry breaking pattern of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{441}
, the
{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{1}
gauge coupling is normalized as
\alpha_{1}=\frac{16}{15}\alpha_{X_{0}}
in the above non-maximal symmetry breaking patterns of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}/\mathfrak{g}_{351}
.
Additionally, there can also be a possible symmetry breaking pattern of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{621}\equiv{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(6)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(2)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{0}}
, with the sequential breaking of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(6)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{0}}\to...\to{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{c}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{Y}
in the extended strong sector.
However, this turns out to be a no-go pattern, since a pair of vectorlike quarks of
(\mathfrak{u}\,,\mathfrak{d})
remain massless through this symmetry breaking pattern.

The possible
d=5
gravity-induced operator of

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\rm HSW}=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{c_{\rm HSW}}{M_{\rm pl}}\Tr\left[\mathbf{63_{H}}U_{\mu\nu}U^{\mu\nu}\right]\,.

(14)

suggested by Hill-Shafi-Wetterich
[
22
,
23
]
could have effect to the gauge coupling unification.
Here, the
U_{\mu\nu}
represents the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)
field strength tensor.
After the GUT-scale symmetry breaking with the VEVs in Eq. (
11
), this HSW operator shifts the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(N_{s})_{s}
,
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(N_{W})_{W}
, and
{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{0}}
field strengths for different symmetry breaking patterns as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{3}{4}\epsilon\right)\Tr\left[G_{\mu\nu}G^{\mu\nu}\right]-\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{5}{4}\epsilon\right)\Tr\left[W_{\mu\nu}W^{\mu\nu}\right]

(15a)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\right)\frac{15}{16}X_{0\mu\nu}X_{0}^{\mu\nu}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{351}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{5}{4}\epsilon\right)\Tr\left[G_{\mu\nu}G^{\mu\nu}\right]-\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{3}{4}\epsilon\right)\Tr\left[W_{\mu\nu}W^{\mu\nu}\right]

(15b)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\right)\frac{15}{16}X_{0\mu\nu}X_{0}^{\mu\nu}\,,

where
\epsilon\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{15}}c_{\rm HSW}\zeta_{0}
with
\zeta_{0}\equiv\frac{v_{U}}{M_{\rm pl}}
.
Consequently, the gauge coupling unification is modified into

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3}{4}\epsilon\right)\alpha_{5s}(v_{U})=\left(1+\frac{5}{4}\epsilon\right)\alpha_{3W}(v_{U})=\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\right)\frac{1}{4}\alpha_{1}(v_{U})=\alpha_{U}(v_{U})\,,

(16a)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{351}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{5}{4}\epsilon\right)\alpha_{3s}(v_{U})=\left(1+\frac{3}{4}\epsilon\right)\alpha_{5W}(v_{U})=\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\right)\frac{1}{4}\alpha_{1}(v_{U})=\alpha_{U}(v_{U})\,,

(16b)

with the affine levels in Eq. (
12a
) and the gravity-induced operator in Eq. (
14
) taken into account.

The rest of the paper will be devoted to analyze the symmetry breaking patterns in Eqs. (
11b
) and (
11c
), with the focus on the gauge coupling evolutions in terms of the corresponding RGEs.
In Secs.
2
,
3
,
4
, and
5
, we analyze the sequential symmetry breaking patterns following the breaking of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}
and
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{351}
at the GUT scale.
Particularly, we derive the RGEs along each symmetry breaking pattern and find that the gauge coupling unification can be achieved in the context of the affine Lie algebra of
\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1}
.
In Sec.
6
, we prove that the symmetry breaking pattern in Eq. (
11c
) is unrealistic, since the Yukawa coupling of
\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{63_{H}}\mathbf{28_{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}
in Eq. (
6
) leads to vanishing mass terms for vectorlike fermions of
(\mathfrak{u}\,,\mathfrak{d})
within the
\mathbf{56_{F}}
.
We summarize the results in Sec.
7
.
In Appendix
A
, we list the generic results of the one- and two-loop
\beta
coefficients.
In Appendices
B
,
C
,
D
, and
E
, we present the details of all possible symmetry breaking patterns after the GUT-scale symmetry breaking of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}/\mathfrak{g}_{351}
.
By reproducing the SM quark/lepton mass hierarchies and the CKM mixing pattern in Ref.
[
6
]
, we identify the SM flavors as well as determine the intermediate symmetry breaking scales for each pattern.
Accordingly, the intermediate symmetry breaking scales are used for the RGEs in Secs.
2
,
3
,
4
, and
5

By following the SSW symmetry breaking pattern in Eq. (
13a
), we tabulate the fermion representations at various stages in Tables
2
,
3
, and
4
.
Along this pattern, the global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T}
symmetry evolves to the global
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{B-L}
at the EW scale according to the following sequence:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{531}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{T}^{\prime}\equiv\mathcal{T}-5t\mathcal{X}_{0}\,,\quad\mathfrak{g}_{431}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{331}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}+8t\mathcal{X}_{2}\,,\quad\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathcal{B}-\mathcal{L}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}\,.

(17)

2.2
Decompositions of the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}
(8) Higgs fields

We decompose the Higgs fields in Eq. (
6
) into components that can develop VEVs for the sequential symmetry breaking stages in Eq. (
13a
).
For Higgs fields of
\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}
, they read

\displaystyle\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}}\oplus\langle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\rangle

(18)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}}\oplus\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\rangle

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\,.

For Higgs fields of
\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}
, they read

\displaystyle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{10}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{2}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}

(19)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\Big[\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\Big]\oplus\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\rangle

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\Big[\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\rangle\oplus\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\rangle\Big]\,.

For Higgs field of
\mathbf{70_{H}}
, it reads

\displaystyle\mathbf{70_{H}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{4}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{4}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{10}}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}

(20)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}}}\supset\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}}\supset\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}\rangle\,.

2.3
RGEs of the SSW symmetry breaking pattern

Between the
v_{531}\leq\mu\leq v_{U}
, the massless Higgs fields are summarized as follows:

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{,\omega}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{10}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{2}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{,\dot{\omega}}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{4}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{4}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{10}}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}\subset\mathbf{70_{H}}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{4}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{4}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{10}}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{70_{H}}}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\omega}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\omega}\subset\mathbf{8_{H}}^{\omega}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{2}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\subset\mathbf{28_{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\,.

(21)

All fermionic components of
\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\Omega}\oplus\mathbf{28_{F}}\oplus\mathbf{56_{F}}
remain massless after the decomposition into the
\mathfrak{g}_{531}
IRs.
Consequently, we have the two-loop
\mathfrak{g}_{531}

\beta
coefficients of

\displaystyle(b^{(1)}_{{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(5)_{s}}\,,b^{(1)}_{{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{W}}\,,b^{(1)}_{{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{0}}})=(+54\,,+60\,,+\frac{368}{5})\,,

\displaystyle b^{(2)}_{\mathfrak{g}_{531}}=\begin{pmatrix}6984/5&216&672/25\\
648&728&352/15\\
16128/25&2816/15&98816/1125\end{pmatrix}\,.

(22)

Between the
v_{431}\leq\mu\leq v_{531}
, the massless Higgs fields are the following:

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3,\rm V,\rm VI}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3,\rm V,\rm VI}\subset...\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,3,\rm V,\rm VI}\,,

(23)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus\Big[(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\Big]\subset...\subset\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}\,,\quad\dot{\omega}=(\dot{1},\dot{2},\dot{\rm VII},\dot{\rm VIII},\dot{\rm IX})\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\rm VII}}\oplus\Big[(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\rm VII}}\oplus(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,+\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\rm VII}}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle\Big[(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\rm VII}}\oplus(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\rm VII}}\Big]\subset...\subset\mathbf{28_{H}}^{\dot{\rm VII}}\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}}\subset...\subset\mathbf{70_{H}}\,.

All
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}
(8) fermions in Eq. (
86
) remain massless after the decomposition into the
\mathfrak{g}_{431}
IRs.
Consequently, we have the two-loop
\mathfrak{g}_{431}

\beta
coefficients of

\displaystyle(b^{(1)}_{{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{s}}\,,b^{(1)}_{{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{W}}\,,b^{(1)}_{{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{1}}})=(+\frac{5}{3}\,,+\frac{11}{2}\,,+\frac{115}{6})\,,

\displaystyle b^{(2)}_{\mathfrak{g}_{431}}=\begin{pmatrix}3511/12&76&269/24\\
285/2&233&47/4\\
1345/8&94&5267/144\end{pmatrix}\,.

(24)

The
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(5)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{0}}
gauge couplings match with the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{1}}
gauge couplings according to the following conditions:

\displaystyle\alpha_{5s}^{-1}(v_{531})=\alpha_{4s}^{-1}(v_{531})\,,\penalty\ \alpha_{X_{1}}^{-1}(v_{531})=\frac{1}{10}\alpha_{5s}^{-1}(v_{531})+\alpha_{X_{0}}^{-1}(v_{531})\,.

(25)

Between the
v_{331}\leq\mu\leq v_{431}
, the massless Higgs fields are

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3,\rm VI}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3,\rm VI}\subset...\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{,3,\rm VI}\,,

(26)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus\Big[(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,0)_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\Big]\subset...\subset\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}\,,\quad\dot{\omega}=(\dot{2},\dot{\rm VIII},\dot{\rm IX})\,,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}\subset...\subset\mathbf{70_{H}}\,.

All
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}
(8) fermions in Eq. (
90
) remain massless after the decomposition into the
\mathfrak{g}_{331}
IRs.

\mathfrak{g}_{331}

\beta

\displaystyle b^{(2)}_{\mathfrak{g}_{331}}=\begin{pmatrix}256/3&36&58/9\\

{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(4)_{s}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{1}}

{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{c}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{2}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{4s}^{-1}(v_{431})=\alpha_{3c}^{-1}(v_{431})\,,\penalty\ \alpha_{X_{2}}^{-1}(v_{431})=\frac{1}{6}\alpha_{4s}^{-1}(v_{431})+\alpha_{X_{1}}^{-1}(v_{431})\,.

(28)

Between the
v_{\rm EW}\leq\mu\leq v_{331}
, the massless Higgs field is

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}\subset(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}\subset(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}}\subset(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle\subset\mathbf{70_{H}}\,.

(29)

{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}
(8) fermions in Eq. (
94
) remain massless after the decomposition into the
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}
IRs.

\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}

\displaystyle b^{(2)}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}}=\begin{pmatrix}-26&9/2&11/6\\

44/3&9/2&199/18\end{pmatrix}\,.

(30)

The
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(3)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{X_{2}}
gauge couplings of
(\alpha_{3W}\,,\alpha_{X_{2}})
match with the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(2)_{W}\oplus{\mathfrak{u}}(1)_{\rm Y}

\displaystyle\alpha_{3W}^{-1}(v_{331})=\alpha_{2W}^{-1}(v_{331})\,,\penalty\ \alpha_{Y}^{-1}(v_{331})=\frac{1}{3}\alpha_{3W}^{-1}(v_{331})+\alpha_{X_{2}}^{-1}(v_{331})\,.

).

3.2
Decompositions of the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}
(8) Higgs fields

We decompose the Higgs fields in Eq. (
6
) into components that can be responsible for the sequential symmetry breaking stages in Eq. (

).
For Higgs fields of
\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}
, they read

\displaystyle\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,\omega}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}}\oplus\langle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\rangle

(34)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\rangle\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega}\rangle\,.

For Higgs fields of
\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}
, they read

\displaystyle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{10}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{2}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}

(35)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\Big[\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\rangle\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\Big]\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\Big[\underline{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}\rangle\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\oplus

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\rangle\,.

For Higgs field of
\mathbf{70_{H}}
, it reads

\displaystyle\mathbf{70_{H}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{4}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{4}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}\oplus\underline{(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}}\oplus(\mathbf{\overline{10}}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}

(36)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}}}\supset\underline{(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}}}\supset\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}\rangle\,.

10^{3}\,{\rm GeV}\lesssim\mu\lesssim 10^{13}\,{\rm GeV}
are zoomed out in order to highlight the behaviors in three intermediate symmetry breaking scales given by the benchmark point in Eq. (
216
).

With the one- and two-loop
\beta
coefficients defined in Eqs. (
5.3
), (
5.3
), (
5.3
), and (
2.3
), we plot the RGEs of the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)
setup along the WWW symmetry breaking pattern in Fig.
4
.
The discontinuities of the
\mathfrak{u}(1)
gauge couplings at three intermediate scales follow from their definitions in Eqs. (
69
), (
72
), and (
73
).
The benchmark point in Fig.
4
is marked by
\star
and reads

\displaystyle c_{\rm HSW}\approx 4.16\,,\penalty\ v_{U}\approx 4.42\times 10^{17}\,{\rm GeV}\,,

\displaystyle\alpha_{3c}^{-1}(v_{U})\approx 25.41\,,\penalty\ \alpha_{5W}^{-1}(v_{U})\approx 38.75\,,\penalty\ \alpha_{X_{0}}^{-1}(v_{U})\approx 8.11\,.

(74)

6
The no-go pattern of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{621}

In this section, we prove that the non-maximal symmetry breaking pattern of
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{621}
cannot be realistic, since the vectorlike quarks of
(\mathfrak{u}\,,\mathfrak{d})
from the
\mathbf{56_{F}}
are massless at the EWSB scale.
For this purpose, we only tabulate the fermion representation of
\mathbf{56_{F}}
at various stages in Table
14
by following the SSS symmetry breaking pattern in Eq. (
11c
).

{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)

Analogous the other symmetry breaking patterns analyzed before, the potential vectorlike fermion mass terms are due to the
d=5
operator from the superpotential in Eq. (
6
), where a Higgs field of
\mathbf{28_{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}
is involved.
The corresponding decompositions are the following:

\displaystyle\mathbf{28_{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{15}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}}\oplus(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}

(75)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\underline{(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{2}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}}\oplus\langle(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\rangle

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\oplus\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\rangle\oplus\langle(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\prime\,\dot{\omega}}\rangle\,.

Accordingly, we decompose the Yukawa coupling along the SSS symmetry breaking pattern between two
\mathbf{56_{F}}
’s due to the following
d=5
operator from the superpotential in Eq. (
6
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M_{\rm pl}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\langle\mathbf{63_{H}}\rangle\mathbf{28_{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}

(76)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}(\mathbf{15}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}\otimes(\mathbf{15}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{6})_{\mathbf{F}}\otimes(\mathbf{15}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{F}}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{F}}\otimes\langle(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\rangle

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}(\mathfrak{u}_{L}{\mathfrak{u}_{R}}^{c}-\mathfrak{d}_{L}{\mathfrak{d}_{R}}^{c})w_{\mathbf{5}}^{\dot{\omega}}\,,

where we take
\langle(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}\rangle\equiv w_{\mathbf{5}}^{\dot{\omega}}
and the decompositions of the Higgs fields of
\mathbf{28_{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}
in Eq. (
75
) are used.
To see why this term vanishes, let us denote relevant fields in terms of their components as
\Psi_{\alpha}^{\left[\bar{a}\,\bar{b}\right]\,,i}\equiv(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{F}}
and
\Phi^{\bar{e}\,,\dot{\omega}}\equiv(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}
, with
\bar{a}=1\,,...\,,5
being the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(5)_{s}
indices,
i=1\,,2
being the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(2)_{W}
indices, and
\alpha=1\,,2
being the two-component Weyl spinor indices.
The above gauge-invariant Yukawa coupling term can be explicitly expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\epsilon_{\bar{a}\bar{b}\bar{c}\bar{d}\bar{e}}{\epsilon_{ij}}\,\Psi_{\alpha}^{\left[\bar{a}\,\bar{b}\right]\,,i}\Psi_{\beta}^{\left[\bar{c}\,\bar{d}\right]\,,j}\Phi^{\bar{e}\,,\dot{\omega}}=\epsilon^{\beta\alpha}\epsilon_{\bar{c}\bar{d}\bar{a}\bar{b}\bar{e}}{\epsilon_{ji}}\,\Psi_{\beta}^{\left[\bar{c}\,\bar{d}\right]\,,j}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\left[\bar{a}\,\bar{b}\right]\,,i}\Phi^{\bar{e}\,,\dot{\omega}}

(77)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\epsilon_{\bar{a}\bar{b}\bar{c}\bar{d}\bar{e}}{\epsilon_{ij}}\,\Psi_{\beta}^{\left[\bar{c}\,\bar{d}\right]\,,j}\Psi_{\alpha}^{\left[\bar{a}\,\bar{b}\right]\,,i}\Phi^{\bar{e}\,,\dot{\omega}}=-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\epsilon_{\bar{a}\bar{b}\bar{c}\bar{d}\bar{e}}{\epsilon_{ij}}\,\Psi_{\alpha}^{\left[\bar{a}\,\bar{b}\right]\,,i}\Psi_{\beta}^{\left[\bar{c}\,\bar{d}\right]\,,j}\Phi^{\bar{e}\,,\dot{\omega}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{F}}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{F}}\otimes(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,-\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dot{\omega}}=0\,,

where we have swapped all gauge and spinor indices of two Weyl fermions in the second line.
Therefore, two vectorlike fermions of
(\mathfrak{u}\,,\mathfrak{d})
remain massless along the
{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)\to\mathfrak{g}_{621}
symmetry breaking pattern.

Table 15:
The benchmark points of the affine Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1}
through four possible non-maximal symmetry breaking patterns with the
\mathcal{N}=1
SUSY extension.

In this paper, we focus on the possible non-maximal symmetry breaking patterns of the affine Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1}\to\mathfrak{g}_{531}/\mathfrak{g}_{351}
allowed by the SUSY extension.
Based on the analyses to the two-loop RGEs of each pattern, we found that the gauge coupling unification can be achieved below the reduced Planck scale according to the relations given in Eqs. (
16
) in the context of the affine Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1}
.
The corresponding unification scales and the unified gauge couplings have been obtained and summarized in Table
15
, with the effects from the
d=5
gravity-induced HSW operator taken into account.
By using the naive estimation
[
24
,
25
]
of

\displaystyle\tau[p\to\pi^{0}+e^{+}]

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{v_{U}^{4}}{m_{p}^{5}}\,,\quad m_{p}=938\,{\rm MeV}\,,

(78)

purely from the gauge sector contribution, we have found significantly enhanced proton lifetime of
\tau[p\to\pi^{0}+e^{+}]\gtrsim\mathcal{O}(10^{71})\,{\rm yrs}
, even without considering the mixing effects.
This means several ongoing experimental probes of the proton decay modes are unlikely to discover such a signal, with the typical exclusion limit of
\sim{\cal O}(10^{34})\,{\rm yrs}
from the Super-Kamiokande
[
26
]
or DUNE Collaboration
[
27
]
.
In addition, we have also proved that the non-maximal symmetry breaking pattern of
\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)_{k_{U}=1}\to\mathfrak{g}_{621}
is not realistic, since the vectorlike quarks of
(\mathfrak{u}\,,\mathfrak{d})
within the
\mathbf{56_{F}}
remain massless due to the antisymmetric properties of the corresponding Weyl spinors.

Besides of the gauge coupling unifications, the flavor identifications and the intermediate scales for the specific symmetry breaking pattern have been analyzed in Appendices
B
,
C
,
D
, and
E
.
Among them, there are several differences in the corresponding mass spectra.

(i)

The vectorlike fermions of
(\mathfrak{E}\,,\mathfrak{U}\,,\mathfrak{u}\,,\mathfrak{d})
within the
\mathbf{56_{F}}
may obtain their masses at different stages along different patterns, which are summarized in Tables
16
,
21
,
26
, and
31
.

(ii)

Within the
\mathbf{56_{F}}
, we found different SM flavor identifications of the first- and second-generational SM fermions along the SSW, SWS, and the WSS symmetry breaking patterns, which can be found in Tables
4
,
7
, and
10
.
Meanwhile, the third-generational SM fermions of
(t_{L}\,,b_{L}\,,{t_{R}}^{c}\,,{\tau_{R}}^{c})
always live in the
\mathbf{28_{F}}
, regardless of the specific symmetry breaking patterns.

(iii)

Given the SM flavor identifications along the SWS and WSS symmetry breaking patterns, we have already observed the tree-level mass splitting between the strange quark and the muon.
The origin of the Cabbibo angle is interpreted as the ratio of
\zeta_{23}
between two different symmetry breaking scales along the SWS and WSS patterns.

Above all, to determine the realistic symmetry breaking patterns, one should rely on the RGEs of the corresponding SM fermion mass terms based on the current results together with the previous ones along the maximal symmetry breaking patterns in Refs.
[
6
,
10
]
.

Appendix A
The gauge coupling unification in the
\widehat{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}}(8)
theory

Generically, the two-loop RGE of a gauge coupling of
\alpha_{\Upsilon}
in the
\overline{\rm MS}
scheme for the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\Upsilon}
is given by
[
28
]

\frac{\mathrm{d}\alpha^{-1}_{\Upsilon}\left(\mu\right)}{\mathrm{d}\log\mu}=-\frac{b^{\left(1\right)}_{\Upsilon}}{2\pi}-\sum_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}\frac{b^{\left(2\right)}_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}}{8\pi^{2}\alpha^{-1}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}\left(\mu\right)}\,.

(79)

The non-SUSY one- and two-loop
\beta
coefficients for the non-Abelian Lie algebras are given by

\displaystyle b^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{11}{3}C_{2}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Upsilon})+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{F}T(\mathcal{R}^{F}_{\Upsilon})+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{S}T(\mathcal{R}^{S}_{\Upsilon})\,,

(80a)

\displaystyle b^{(2)}_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{34}{3}\delta_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}C_{2}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Upsilon})^{2}+\sum_{F}\Big[2\sum_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}C_{2}(\mathcal{R}^{F}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})+\frac{10}{3}\delta_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}C_{2}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Upsilon})\Big]T(\mathcal{R}^{F}_{\Upsilon})

(80b)

\displaystyle+\sum_{S}\Big[4\sum_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}C_{2}(\mathcal{R}^{S}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})+\frac{2}{3}\delta_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}C_{2}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Upsilon})\Big]T(\mathcal{R}^{S}_{\Upsilon})\,.

For the Abelian Lie algebra with charges denoted as
\mathcal{X}^{F/S}_{\Upsilon}
, the one- and two-loop
\beta
coefficients are

\displaystyle b^{\left(1\right)}_{\Upsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}\sum_{F}\left(\mathcal{X}^{F}_{\Upsilon}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{S}(\mathcal{X}^{S}_{\Upsilon})^{2}\,,

(81a)

\displaystyle b^{\left(2\right)}_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{F\,,\Upsilon^{\prime}}(\mathcal{X}^{F}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})^{2}\cdot(\mathcal{X}^{F}_{\Upsilon})^{2}+4\sum_{S\,,\Upsilon^{\prime}}(\mathcal{X}^{S}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})^{2}\cdot(\mathcal{X}^{S}_{\Upsilon})^{2}\,.

(81b)

We will also assume a set of SUSY RGEs between the first stage, and the corresponding one- and two-loop
\beta
coefficients are given by

\displaystyle b^{(1)}_{\Upsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-3C_{2}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Upsilon})+\sum_{\Phi}T(\mathcal{R}^{\Phi}_{\Upsilon})\,,

(82a)

\displaystyle b^{(2)}_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-6\delta_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}C_{2}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Upsilon})^{2}+\sum_{\Phi}\Big[4\sum_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}C_{2}(\mathcal{R}^{\Phi}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})+2\delta_{\Upsilon\Upsilon^{\prime}}C_{2}(\mathcal{G}_{\Upsilon})\Big]T(\mathcal{R}^{\Phi}_{\Upsilon})\,.

(82b)

In practice, we derive the two-loop RGEs numerically by using the PyR@TE code
[
29
]
.

Appendix B
The intermediate stages of the SSW symmetry breaking pattern

Schematically, we assign the Higgs VEVs according to the symmetry breaking pattern in Eq. (
13a
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{531}\to\mathfrak{g}_{431}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm IV}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}W_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,{\rm IV}}\,,

(83a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{431}\to\mathfrak{g}_{331}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm V}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,{\rm V}}\,,\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}\,,

(83b)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{331}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\,,

(83c)

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}\,,\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm IX}}^{\prime}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm IX}}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle{\rm EWSB}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}v_{\rm EW}\,.

(83d)

For our later convenience, we also parametrize different symmetry breaking VEVs in terms of the following dimensionless quantities:

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}\equiv\frac{W_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,{\rm IV}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\zeta_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,{\rm V}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,3,{\rm VI}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm IX}}^{\prime}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}\gg\zeta_{1}\gg\zeta_{2}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{2}\gg\zeta_{3}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}\,,\quad\zeta_{ij}\equiv\frac{\zeta_{j}}{\zeta_{i}}\,,\quad(i<j)\,.

(84)

, we summarize all vectorlike fermions that become massive during different stages of the SSW symmetry breaking pattern.

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{5}{12})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{2M_{\rm pl}m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}}^{2}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{3}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\Big]

\displaystyle\otimes

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\prime}\rangle^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}}{4}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\Big[\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}^{2}}\left(d_{L}{d_{R}}^{c}+e_{L}{e_{R}}^{c}\right)+\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}^{2}}\left(d_{L}{s_{R}}^{c}+\mu_{L}{e_{R}}^{c}\right)\Big]v_{\rm EW}+H.c.\,,

via the Higgs mixing operator of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{B}}^{d=5}
in Eq. (
8b
).
For convenience, we parametrize the following ratios of

\displaystyle\Delta_{\dot{\omega}}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}^{2}}\,,\quad\Delta_{\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}^{2}}\,.

(101)

With the Higgs VEVs assignments in Eq. (
83
), we can obtain the following propagator masses of

\displaystyle m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}\sim m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}\sim\mathcal{O}(v_{431})\,,

\displaystyle m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}\sim m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{12})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}\sim\mathcal{O}(v_{331})\,.

(102)

B.6
The SM quark/lepton masses and the benchmark

For all up-type quarks with
Q_{e}=+\frac{2}{3}
, we write down the following tree-level masses from both the renormalizable Yukawa couplings and the gravity-induced terms in the basis of
\mathcal{U}\equiv(u\,,c\,,t\,)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&c_{4}\dot{\zeta}_{2}/\sqrt{2}&c_{5}\zeta_{1}/\sqrt{2}\\
c_{4}\dot{\zeta}_{2}/\sqrt{2}&0&c_{5}\zeta_{2}/\sqrt{2}\\
c_{5}\zeta_{1}/\sqrt{2}&c_{5}\zeta_{2}/\sqrt{2}&Y_{\mathcal{T}}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,.

For all down-type quarks with
Q_{e}=-\frac{1}{3}
, we find the following tree-level mass matrix in the basis of
\mathcal{D}\equiv(d\,,s\,,b\,)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{D}}\approx\frac{1}{4}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}\zeta_{3}^{\prime}&0\\
Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}^{\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}^{\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&0\\
0&0&Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}\zeta_{23}^{-1}\zeta_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,,

where we interpret the ratio between two
\mathfrak{g}_{331}
VEVs as the Cabibbo angle of
\tan\lambda\equiv\frac{\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}}{\dot{\zeta}_{3}}
.
For all charged leptons with
Q_{e}=-1
, their tree-level mass matrix is the transposition of the down-type quark mass matrix and expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathcal{M}^{T}_{\mathcal{D}}\approx\frac{1}{4}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}^{\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&0\\
Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}\zeta_{3}^{\prime}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}^{\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&0\\
0&0&Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}\zeta_{23}^{-1}\zeta_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,.

Based on the above SM quark/lepton mass matrices, we find the following benchmark point of

\displaystyle v_{531}\simeq 1.4\times 10^{17}\,\mathrm{GeV}\,,\quad v_{431}\simeq 4.8\times 10^{15}\,\mathrm{GeV}\,,\quad v_{331}\simeq 4.8\times 10^{13}\,\mathrm{GeV}\,,

(115)

to reproduce the observed hierarchical masses as well as the CKM mixing pattern, as summarized in Table
20
.

Appendix C
The intermediate stages of the SWS symmetry breaking pattern

Schematically, we assign the Higgs VEVs according to the symmetry breaking pattern as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{531}\to\mathfrak{g}_{431}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm IV}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}W_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,{\rm IV}}\,,

(116a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{431}\to\mathfrak{g}_{421}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm V}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,{\rm V}}\,,\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}\,,

(116b)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{421}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\,,

(116c)

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}\,,\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm IX}}^{\prime}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm IX}}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle{\rm EWSB}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}v_{\rm EW}\,.

(116d)

For our later convenience, we also parametrize different symmetry breaking VEVs in terms of the following dimensionless quantities:

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}\equiv\frac{W_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,{\rm IV}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\zeta_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,{\rm V}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,3,{\rm VI}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm IX}}^{\prime}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}\gg\zeta_{1}\gg\zeta_{2}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{2}\gg\zeta_{3}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}\,,\quad\zeta_{ij}\equiv\frac{\zeta_{j}}{\zeta_{i}}\,,\quad(i<j)\,.

(117)

C.6
The SM quark/lepton masses and the benchmark

For all up-type quarks with
Q_{e}=+\frac{2}{3}
, we write down the following tree-level masses from both the renormalizable Yukawa couplings and the gravity-induced terms in the basis of
\mathcal{U}\equiv(u\,,c\,,t\,)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&c_{5}\zeta_{1}/\sqrt{2}\\
c_{4}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}/\sqrt{2}&0&c_{5}\zeta_{3}/\sqrt{2}\\
c_{5}\zeta_{1}/\sqrt{2}&c_{5}\zeta_{2}/\sqrt{2}&Y_{\mathcal{T}}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,.

For all down-type quarks with
Q_{e}=-\frac{1}{3}
, we find the following tree-level mass matrix in the basis of
\mathcal{D}\equiv(d\,,s\,,b\,)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{D}}\approx\frac{1}{4}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&0\\
Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}^{\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{2}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}^{\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{2}&0\\
0&0&Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}\zeta_{23}^{-1}\zeta_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,.

For all charged leptons with
Q_{e}=-1
, their tree-level mass matrix is expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{L}}\approx\frac{1}{4}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}^{\prime\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&0\\
Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}^{\prime\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&0\\
0&0&Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}\zeta_{23}^{-1}\zeta_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,,

in the basis of
\mathcal{L}\equiv(e\,,\mu\,,\tau\,)
.
Based on the above SM quark/lepton mass matrices, we find the following benchmark point of

\displaystyle v_{531}\simeq 1.4\times 10^{17}\,\mathrm{GeV}\,,\quad v_{431}\simeq 4.8\times 10^{15}\,\mathrm{GeV}\,,\quad v_{421}\simeq 1.1\times 10^{15}\,\mathrm{GeV}\,,

(151)

to reproduce the observed hierarchical masses as well as the CKM mixing pattern, as summarized in Table
25
.

Appendix D
The intermediate stages of the WSS symmetry breaking pattern

Schematically, we assign the Higgs VEVs according to the symmetry breaking pattern as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{531}\to\mathfrak{g}_{521}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm IV}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}W_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,{\rm IV}}\,,

(152a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{521}\to\mathfrak{g}_{421}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm V}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,{\rm V}}\,,\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}\,,

(152b)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{421}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,3,\rm{VI}}\,,

(152c)

\displaystyle\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}\,,\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm IX}}^{\prime}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm IX}}^{\prime}\,,

\displaystyle{\rm EWSB}\penalty\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}}\rangle\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}v_{\rm EW}\,.

(152d)

For later convenience, we also parametrize different symmetry breaking VEVs in terms of the following dimensionless quantities:

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}\equiv\frac{W_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,{\rm IV}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\zeta_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,{\rm V}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{2}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1},\dot{\rm VII}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,3,{\rm VI}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{2},\dot{\rm IX}}^{\prime}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{3}\equiv\frac{V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\dot{\rm VIII}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\,,

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}\gg\zeta_{1}\gg\zeta_{2}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{2}\gg\zeta_{3}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\sim\dot{\zeta}_{3}\,,\quad\zeta_{ij}\equiv\frac{\zeta_{j}}{\zeta_{i}}\,,\quad(i<j)\,.

(153)

, we summarize all vectorlike fermions that become massive during different stages of the WSS symmetry breaking pattern.

\mathfrak{g}_{421}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\rm SM}
is achieved by
(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,{\rm VI}}\subset\mathbf{\overline{8_{H}}}_{\,,{\rm VI}}

in the rank-three sector, according to the
\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)_{T^{\prime\prime\prime}}

.

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{B}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{3}{10})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle d_{\mathscr{A}}\frac{W_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,{\rm IV}}}{\sqrt{2}M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes\langle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{2}}^{\dagger}\rangle\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{3}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\,\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{B}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\omega_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle d_{\mathscr{A}}\frac{W_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,{\rm IV}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,{\rm V}}}{2M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\omega_{3}}^{\dagger}\rangle\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}}{4}\frac{W_{\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,{\rm IV}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,{\rm V}}V_{\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,{\rm VI}}}{M_{\rm pl}m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,3}}^{2}}\left(b_{L}{b_{R}}^{c}+\tau_{L}{\tau_{R}}^{c}\right)v_{\rm EW}+H.c.\,,

via the Higgs mixing operator of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{A}}^{d=5}
in Eq. (
8a
).

For the Yukawa coupling of
\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}\times\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{70_{H}}^{\dagger}\left(\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\right)+H.c.

(170)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{10}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{2}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}\otimes\langle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{2M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{10}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{2}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{2M_{\rm pl}m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}}^{2}}(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\otimes\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\prime\dagger}\rangle\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}}{4}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}\Big[\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}^{2}}\left(e_{L}{\mu_{R}}^{c}\right)+\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}^{2}}\left(\mu_{L}{\mu_{R}}^{c}\right)\Big]v_{\rm EW}+H.c.\,,

and

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}\times\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{70_{H}}^{\dagger}\left(\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\right)+H.c.

(171)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{1}{15})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{1}{3})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{7}{15})_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}\otimes\langle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{F}}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{5}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{4}{5})_{\mathbf{F}}\Big]\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{3}{10})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{2M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{3}{10})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\Big[(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\oplus(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+1)_{\mathbf{F}}^{\prime\prime}\Big]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{2M_{\rm pl}m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}}^{2}}\langle(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\rangle\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}}{4}\dot{\zeta}_{3}\Big[\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}^{2}}\left(d_{L}{d_{R}}^{c}+e_{L}{e_{R}}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}^{2}}\left(d_{L}{s_{R}}^{c}+\mu_{L}{e_{R}}^{c}\right)\Big]v_{\rm EW}+H.c.\,,

and

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}\mathbf{\overline{8_{F}}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\mathbf{56_{F}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}\times\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{70_{H}}^{\dagger}\left(\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\right)+H.c.

(172)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle Y_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{1}{15})_{\mathbf{F}}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}}{M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,-\frac{2}{15})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{3}}\,,+\frac{4}{15})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}\otimes\langle\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{1}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{\overline{28_{H}}}_{\,,\dot{\rm VII}}\rangle+H.c.

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{d_{\mathscr{B}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{2M_{\rm pl}}(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{3}{10})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes\langle(\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{5})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{2}}^{\dagger}\rangle\otimes(\mathbf{10}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{10})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\frac{Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{2\sqrt{2}m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}_{1}}}^{2}}\dot{\zeta}_{2}(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{1}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{F}}^{\dot{\omega}_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{6}\,,\mathbf{2}\,,0)_{\mathbf{F}}\otimes(\mathbf{4}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,+\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}}^{\dagger}+H.c.

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}}{4}\dot{\zeta}_{2}\Big[\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}^{2}}\left(s_{L}{d_{R}}^{c}\right)+\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}^{2}}\left(s_{L}{s_{R}}^{c}\right)\Big]v_{\rm EW}+H.c.\,,

via the Higgs mixing operator of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{B}}^{d=5}
in Eq. (
8b
).
For convenience, we parametrize the following ratios of

\displaystyle\Delta_{\dot{\omega}}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}^{2}}\,,\quad\Delta_{\dot{\omega}}^{\prime}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}^{2}}\,,\quad\Delta_{\dot{\omega}}^{\prime\prime}\equiv\frac{w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{1}}w_{\mathbf{\overline{5}}\,,\dot{\rm VII}}}{m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{\omega}}}^{2}}\,.

(173)

152
), we can obtain the following propagator masses of

\displaystyle m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}\sim m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}\sim m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{1}}}\sim\mathcal{O}(v_{431})\,,

\displaystyle m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{2})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}\sim m_{(\mathbf{\overline{4}}\,,\mathbf{\overline{2}}\,,-\frac{1}{4})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}\sim m_{(\mathbf{1}\,,\mathbf{3}\,,-\frac{2}{3})_{\mathbf{H}\,,\dot{2}}}\sim\mathcal{O}(v_{421})\,.

(174)

D.6
The SM quark/lepton masses and the benchmark

For all up-type quarks with
Q_{e}=+\frac{2}{3}
, we write down the following tree-level masses from both the renormalizable Yukawa couplings and the gravity-induced terms in the basis of
\mathcal{U}\equiv(u\,,c\,,t\,)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&c_{5}\zeta_{2}/\sqrt{2}\\
c_{4}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}/\sqrt{2}&0&c_{5}\zeta_{3}/\sqrt{2}\\
c_{5}\zeta_{2}/\sqrt{2}&c_{5}\zeta_{1}/\sqrt{2}&Y_{\mathcal{T}}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,.

For all down-type quarks with
Q_{e}=-\frac{1}{3}
, we find the following tree-level mass matrix in the basis of
\mathcal{D}\equiv(d\,,s\,,b\,)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{D}}\approx\frac{1}{4}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&0\\
Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}^{\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{2}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}^{\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{2}&0\\
0&0&Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}\zeta_{23}^{-1}\zeta_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,.

For all charged leptons with
Q_{e}=-1
, their tree-level mass matrix is expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{L}}\approx\frac{1}{4}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{1}}^{\prime\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&0\\
Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}\dot{\zeta}_{3}&Y_{\mathcal{D}}d_{\mathscr{B}}\Delta_{\dot{2}}^{\prime\prime}\dot{\zeta}_{3}^{\prime}&0\\
0&0&Y_{\mathcal{B}}d_{\mathscr{A}}\zeta_{23}^{-1}\zeta_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)v_{\rm EW}\,,

in the basis of
\mathcal{L}\equiv(e\,,\mu\,,\tau\,)
.
Based on the above SM quark/lepton mass matrices, we find the following benchmark point of

\displaystyle v_{531}\simeq 1.4\times 10^{17}\,\mathrm{GeV}\,,\quad v_{521}\simeq 4.8\times 10^{15}\,\mathrm{GeV}\,,\quad v_{421}\simeq 1.1\times 10^{15}\,\mathrm{GeV}\,,\quad

(187)

to reproduce the observed hierarchical masses as well as the CKM mixing pattern, as summarized in Table
30
.
